#### `commerce.orders`

- **Why:** The order state machine above. The order row lock serialises capture from any trigger (redirect, webhook, sweeper, reconciliation; R9).
- **Owner:** PAY.
- **Features:** PAY-03, PAY-04, PAY-05, EXAM-04.
- **Relations:** `identity.users` 1—N; `products` 1—N; `coupons` 0..1—N; 1—N `payments`; 1—1 `invoices`.
- **Read/write pattern:**
  - About 1 order per 100 DAU per day.
  - Polled by the client while verifying: 1 read every 3 s for up to 2 min.
  - Swept by the pending-order job at 1, 5, 15 and 60 minutes.
- **Indexes:**
  - PK `id`.
  - `ix_orders_status_created (status, created_at) WHERE status IN ('created','pending')`: the sweeper.
  - `ix (user_id, created_at DESC)`: the user's purchase list.
- **Scalability risks:** Low volume. Exam-registration fee spikes (about 20k paid registrations in an hour) give about 6 orders/s, which is trivial.
- **Build:** 6. Build 8 adds `beneficiary_user_id`.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| user_id | uuid | no | — | FK `identity.users(id)` |
| beneficiary_user_id | uuid | yes | — | Build 8 |
| product_id | uuid | no | — | FK `products(id)` |
| amount_poisha | bigint | no | — | CHECK (amount_poisha >= 0) |
| discount_poisha | bigint | no | 0 | CHECK (discount_poisha BETWEEN 0 AND amount_poisha) |
| payable_poisha | bigint | no | — | CHECK (payable_poisha = amount_poisha - discount_poisha) |
| coupon_id | uuid | yes | — | FK `coupons(id)` |
| status | text | no | 'created' | CHECK IN ('created','pending','paid','failed','expired','partially_refunded','refunded') |
| provider | text | yes | — | CHECK IN ('bkash','sslcommerz','aamarpay','google_play','apple','free') |
| expires_at | timestamptz | no | — | created_at + 24 h |
| paid_at | timestamptz | yes | — | — |
| created_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |
| updated_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |

#### `commerce.payments`

- **Why:** One provider payment per attempt to pay an order, following the payment state machine above. The unique provider transaction id makes a double capture impossible across processes.
- **Owner:** PAY.
- **Features:** PAY-03, PAY-05.
- **Relations:** `orders` 1—N (a retry after a failure creates a new payment); 1—N `refunds`.
- **Read/write pattern:** Low volume. Read by the reconciliation job for one business date.
- **Indexes:**
  - PK `id`.
  - uq `(provider, provider_txn_id)`: double-capture guard.
  - uq `(provider, provider_payment_id)`: execute-once guard and IPN lookup.
  - `ix (provider, captured_at)`: reconciliation by business date.
  - `ix (status) WHERE status IN ('initiated','execute_requested','executed','execute_unknown','duplicate_captured')`: the sweeper and the duplicate-refund job.
- **Scalability risks:** None.
- **Build:** 6.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| order_id | uuid | no | — | FK `orders(id)` |
| provider | text | no | — | as orders.provider |
| provider_payment_id | text | yes | — | uq with provider |
| provider_txn_id | text | yes | — | uq with provider (multiple NULLs allowed until known) |
| amount_poisha | bigint | no | — | CHECK (amount_poisha >= 0) |
| status | text | no | 'initiated' | CHECK IN ('initiated','execute_requested','execute_unknown','executed','captured','failed','reversed','duplicate_captured','refunded') |
| execute_requested_at | timestamptz | yes | — | Set and committed before the Execute call; CHECK (status <> 'execute_requested' OR execute_requested_at IS NOT NULL) |
| executed_at | timestamptz | yes | — | — |
| captured_at | timestamptz | yes | — | CHECK (status NOT IN ('captured','reversed') OR captured_at IS NOT NULL) |
| raw_status | jsonb | yes | — | Last provider response (secrets stripped) |
| created_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |
| updated_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |

#### `commerce.provider_events`

- **Why:** The inbox for webhooks, IPNs and store notifications. Each delivery is inserted first under a unique key, so a duplicate gets a 200 with no work done (v2 §7.2).
- **Owner:** PAY.
- **Features:** PAY-05.
- **Relations:** Soft reference to payments through `payload`.
- **Read/write pattern:** One insert per delivery. Processed asynchronously by `pay.provider_event` jobs.
- **Indexes:** PK `id`; uq `(provider, provider_event_id)`; `ix (processed_at) WHERE processed_at IS NULL`.
- **Scalability risks:** Kept 2 years, then archived.
- **Build:** 6.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| provider | text | no | — | — |
| provider_event_id | text | no | — | uq with provider (SNS MessageId, aggregator `tran_id`+`status`, Pub/Sub messageId) |
| kind | text | no | — | — |
| payload | jsonb | no | — | Raw body (PII minimised) |
| signature_ok | boolean | no | — | Unsigned or invalid deliveries are stored with `false` and never processed |
| received_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |
| processed_at | timestamptz | yes | — | — |

#### `commerce.store_purchases`

- **Why:** Server-side verification of Google Play Billing purchase tokens (and Apple IAP transactions from Build 8), feeding the same capture transaction.
- **Owner:** PAY.
- **Features:** PAY-03, OFF-04 (Build 8).
- **Relations:** `identity.users` 1—N; `products` 1—N; `orders` 0..1—1.
- **Read/write pattern:** One row per purchase. Updated by Real-time Developer Notifications.
- **Indexes:** PK `id`; uq `purchase_token_hash`; `ix (user_id)`.
- **Scalability risks:** Purchase tokens are stored only as hashes. The raw token is used for the verification call and then discarded, except where the acknowledge API needs it (kept encrypted until it is acknowledged).
- **Build:** 6. Build 8 adds `apple` to the platform CHECK.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| user_id | uuid | no | — | FK `identity.users(id)` |
| platform | text | no | — | CHECK IN ('google_play') — `'apple'` added in Build 8 |
| purchase_token_hash | bytea | no | — | uq |
| product_id | uuid | no | — | FK `products(id)` |
| order_id | uuid | yes | — | FK `orders(id)` |
| state | text | no | — | CHECK IN ('pending','purchased','acknowledged','cancelled','refunded','expired') |
| verified_at | timestamptz | yes | — | — |
| acknowledged_at | timestamptz | yes | — | — |
| created_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |

#### `commerce.refunds`

- **Why:** Full and partial refunds, following the refund state machine.
- **Owner:** PAY.
- **Features:** PAY-05, OPS-01.
- **Relations:** `payments` 1—N; 1—1 `credit_notes`.
- **Read/write pattern:** Rare.
- **Indexes:** PK `id`; `ix (payment_id)`; `ix (status) WHERE status IN ('requested','submitted')`.
- **Scalability risks:** The total of refunds per payment must not exceed the payment amount. This is enforced in the service under the order lock, by summing completed and submitted refunds.
- **Build:** 6.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| payment_id | uuid | no | — | FK `payments(id)` |
| amount_poisha | bigint | no | — | CHECK (amount_poisha > 0) |
| reason | text | no | — | — |
| status | text | no | 'requested' | CHECK IN ('requested','submitted','completed','rejected','failed') |
| requested_by | uuid | no | — | FK `identity.users(id)` |
| provider_refund_id | text | yes | — | — |
| completed_at | timestamptz | yes | — | — |
| created_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |

#### `commerce.coupons`

- **Why:** Promo codes, validated when the order is created (PAY-04).
- **Owner:** PAY.
- **Features:** PAY-04, GAM-08 (store-issued coupons, Build 9).
- **Relations:** 1—N `coupon_redemptions`, `orders`.
- **Read/write pattern:** Read at validation. The conditional increment at redemption (R11) runs in the capture transaction.
- **Indexes:** PK `id`; uq `code_norm` (upper-case, trimmed).
- **Scalability risks:** A viral coupon makes `redeemed_count` a hot row. The conditional update serialises redemptions, which is acceptable at order volume (under 10/s). If that is exceeded, preallocated per-user coupons would be used instead.
- **Build:** 6.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| code_norm | text | no | — | uq; CHECK (code_norm = upper(code_norm)) |
| kind | text | no | — | CHECK IN ('percent','flat') |
| value | integer | no | — | CHECK ((kind='percent' AND value BETWEEN 1 AND 100) OR (kind='flat' AND value > 0)) — flat in poisha |
| max_redemptions | integer | yes | — | NULL = unlimited |
| redeemed_count | integer | no | 0 | CHECK (max_redemptions IS NULL OR redeemed_count <= max_redemptions) |
| valid_from | timestamptz | no | — | — |
| valid_to | timestamptz | no | — | CHECK (valid_to > valid_from) |
| product_scope | jsonb | no | '{}' | Product ids or kinds |
| status | text | no | 'active' | CHECK IN ('active','paused','expired') |

#### `commerce.coupon_redemptions`

- **Why:** Enforces one redemption per user per coupon, through the PK.
- **Owner:** PAY.
- **Features:** PAY-04.
- **Relations:** `coupons` 1—N; `identity.users` 1—N; `orders` 1—1.
- **Read/write pattern:** Inserted in the capture transaction.
- **Indexes:** PK `(coupon_id, user_id)`.
- **Scalability risks:** None.
- **Build:** 6.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| coupon_id | uuid | no | — | PK part |
| user_id | uuid | no | — | PK part |
| order_id | uuid | no | — | FK `orders(id)` |
| redeemed_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |

#### `commerce.referral_codes`

- **Why:** A referral code per user, shown on share cards (GAM-07).
- **Owner:** PAY.
- **Features:** PAY-04, GAM-07.
- **Relations:** `identity.users` 1—1.
- **Read/write pattern:** Created lazily.
- **Indexes:** PK `user_id`; uq `code`.
- **Scalability risks:** None.
- **Build:** 6.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| user_id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| code | text | no | — | uq |
| created_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |

#### `commerce.referral_credits`

- **Why:** A ledger of credits earned, spent and reversed through referrals.
- **Owner:** PAY.
- **Features:** PAY-04, PAY-07.
- **Relations:** `identity.users` 1—N.
- **Read/write pattern:** Credit earned on the referred user's first capture (key `ref:earn:{referred_user_id}`). Spent as an order discount (key `ref:spend:{order_id}`). Reversed on refund (key `ref:reverse:{refund_id}`).
- **Indexes:** PK `id`; uq `idempotency_key`; `ix (user_id, created_at)`.
- **Scalability risks:** The balance is computed as a `SUM` per user, which is acceptable at fewer than 100 rows per user.
- **Build:** 6.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| user_id | uuid | no | — | FK `identity.users(id)` |
| kind | text | no | — | CHECK IN ('earned','spent','reversed') |
| amount_poisha | bigint | no | — | CHECK ((kind='earned' AND amount_poisha > 0) OR (kind <> 'earned' AND amount_poisha < 0)) |
| source_ref | text | no | — | — |
| idempotency_key | text | no | — | uq |
| created_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |

#### `commerce.invoice_sequences`

- **Why:** Gapless invoice numbering per fiscal year (July to June in Bangladesh), incremented under a row lock inside the capture transaction (R19).
- **Owner:** PAY.
- **Features:** PAY-04.
- **Relations:** None.
- **Read/write pattern:** One update per capture.
- **Indexes:** PK `fiscal_year`.
- **Scalability risks:** This is a hot row, but serialisation is fine below about 50 captures/s. If that is exceeded, the sequence is split per provider prefix.
- **Build:** 6.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| fiscal_year | text | no | — | PK; e.g. `2026-27` |
| last_no | bigint | no | 0 | CHECK (last_no >= 0) |

- **Owner:** PAY.

| amount_poisha | bigint | no | — | CHECK (amount_poisha >= 0) |
| vat_poisha | bigint | no | 0 | CHECK (vat_poisha >= 0) |

#### `commerce.reconciliation_runs`

- **Why:** A daily run at 03:00 Dhaka per provider, comparing the provider's report (API or uploaded settlement file) with `payments` for the previous business date.
- **Owner:** PAY.
- **Features:** PAY-05.
- **Relations:** 1—N `reconciliation_exceptions`.
- **Read/write pattern:** About 3 rows a day.
- **Indexes:** PK `id`; uq `(provider, business_date)`, so a rerun reuses the row.
- **Scalability risks:** None.
- **Build:** 6.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| provider | text | no | — | — |
| business_date | date | no | — | Dhaka date |
| source | text | no | — | CHECK IN ('api','upload') |
| file_key | text | yes | — | CHECK ((source='upload') = (file_key IS NOT NULL)) |
| status | text | no | 'running' | CHECK IN ('running','matched','exceptions','failed') |
| totals | jsonb | yes | — | Counts and poisha totals, both sides |
| started_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |
| finished_at | timestamptz | yes | — | — |

#### `commerce.reconciliation_exceptions`

- **Why:** Mismatches found by reconciliation. None may stay open past 48 h; an alert fires at 24 h.
- **Owner:** PAY.
- **Features:** PAY-05, OPS-01.
- **Relations:** `reconciliation_runs` 1—N; `payments` 0..1—N.
- **Read/write pattern:** Rare.
- **Indexes:** PK `id`; `ix (status, opened_at) WHERE status='open'`.
- **Scalability risks:** None.
- **Build:** 6.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| run_id | uuid | no | — | FK `reconciliation_runs(id)` |
| kind | text | no | — | CHECK IN ('paid_not_captured','captured_not_paid','amount_mismatch','refund_mismatch','reversed') |
| payment_id | uuid | yes | — | FK `payments(id)` |
| provider_txn_id | text | yes | — | — |
| details | jsonb | no | — | — |
| status | text | no | 'open' | CHECK IN ('open','auto_resolved','resolved','written_off') |
| opened_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |
| resolved_at | timestamptz | yes | — | — |
| resolved_by | uuid | yes | — | — |

| granted_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |

### 4.13 `parent`

#### `parent.guardian_links`

- **Why:** The parent–child link. One parent can link many children, and either side can revoke it (AUTH-07, v2 §7.3). The guardian consent record from NFR-07 can seed a link (`created_via='consent'`) once the guardian signs up.
- **Owner:** PAR. AUTH-07 data lives here (see `00`).
- **Features:** AUTH-07, PAR-01–05, PAY-04 (family), AI-06 (mode lock).
- **Relations:** `identity.users` (guardian) 1—N; `identity.users` (child) 1—N; `identity.guardian_consents` 0..1—1.
- **Read/write pattern:** Rare writes. Read on every guardian request; cached for 60 s.
- **Indexes:**
  - PK `id`.
  - uq partial `(guardian_user_id, child_user_id) WHERE status='active'`.
  - `ix (child_user_id) WHERE status='active'`: child-side checks such as controls and reports.
- **Scalability risks:** None.
- **Build:** 8.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| guardian_user_id | uuid | no | — | FK `identity.users(id)` |
| child_user_id | uuid | no | — | FK `identity.users(id)`; CHECK (guardian_user_id <> child_user_id) |
| status | text | no | 'active' | CHECK IN ('active','revoked') |
| created_via | text | no | — | CHECK IN ('code','qr','consent') |
| consent_id | uuid | yes | — | FK `identity.guardian_consents(id)` |
| created_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |
| revoked_at | timestamptz | yes | — | CHECK ((status='revoked') = (revoked_at IS NOT NULL)) |
| revoked_by | uuid | yes | — | — |

#### `parent.link_codes`

- **Why:** Single-use codes or QR payloads issued in the child's account.
- **Owner:** PAR.
- **Features:** AUTH-07.
- **Relations:** `identity.users` (child) 1—N.
- **Read/write pattern:** Rare. Expires after 24 h.
- **Indexes:** PK `id`; uq `code_hash`; `ix (child_user_id, created_at DESC)`.
- **Scalability risks:** Codes are hashed with HMAC and redemption is rate-limited; there are 5 failures per guardian per hour before lockout.
- **Build:** 8.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| child_user_id | uuid | no | — | — |
| code_hash | bytea | no | — | uq |
| expires_at | timestamptz | no | — | — |
| used_at | timestamptz | yes | — | — |
| created_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |

#### `parent.parent_controls`

- **Why:** Tutor mode lock, daily time cap and quiet hours, enforced through the AI (mode), ANL (time) and PLAN (quiet hours) interfaces.
- **Owner:** PAR.
- **Features:** PAR-03, AI-06, PLAN-02 quiet hours.
- **Relations:** `identity.users` (child) 1—1. If several guardians are linked, the last write wins, and the setter is recorded and audited.
- **Read/write pattern:**
  - Read by the AI gateway on each tutor request (cached for 60 s).
  - Read by PRAC at session start for the time-cap check (cached).
- **Indexes:** PK `child_user_id`.
- **Scalability risks:** The cache invalidates on `parent.controls.changed`.
- **Build:** 8.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| child_user_id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| tutor_mode_lock | text | no | 'none' | CHECK IN ('none','socratic','direct') |
| daily_time_cap_min | smallint | yes | — | CHECK (daily_time_cap_min BETWEEN 15 AND 600) |
| quiet_start | time | yes | — | Dhaka local time |
| quiet_end | time | yes | — | CHECK ((quiet_start IS NULL) = (quiet_end IS NULL)) |
| set_by | uuid | no | — | — |
| updated_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |

#### `parent.weekly_reports`

- **Why:** The weekly report shown in the app, plus a Bangla SMS summary.
- **Owner:** PAR.
- **Features:** PAR-02, PLAN-06.
- **Relations:** One row per guardian, per child, per week.
- **Read/write pattern:** A weekly job on Saturday morning: about 1 row per active link per week.
- **Indexes:** PK `id`; uq `(guardian_user_id, child_user_id, week_key)`, so the job is idempotent.
- **Scalability risks:** SMS cost is about 2 segments × ৳0.35 per report, so SMS is opt-in.
- **Build:** 8.

- **Why:** Guardian-defined alerts, such as "no study for 3 days" or "score dropped".
- **Owner:** PAR.

A constraint would block a later rule version that raises the cap, and contradicts `04` §4 rule 6. |

### 4.14 `exam` (EXAM)

Shared notes for this schema:

- **Rate stages.** "Stage 1" is the launch exam cap of 20,000 registrants (C17). "200k" is the NFR-01 design ceiling. Rates for the ingest path come from v2 §6.1.
- **Partitioned tables.** `exam_answer_batches` and `exam_answers_latest` are partitioned by RANGE on `exam_starts_on`, with monthly partitions. The value is copied from `exams.starts_at` converted to its Dhaka date when the attempt is created, so it is fixed for the life of the attempt. Every unique key on these tables includes it (01 §1.2).
- **Writers.**
  - Only `exam-consumers` writes the answer log.
  - `exam-ingest` never touches Postgres; this is enforced by `.importlinter`.
  - `api` writes everything else in this schema.

#### Exam state machine (`exams.status`)

```
draft ──schedule──▶ scheduled ──freeze-paper (T−24h)──▶ paper_frozen ──starts_at──▶ live
  │                    │                                    │                       │
  └──cancel──▶ cancelled ◀──cancel (before starts_at)───────┘                       │ close_base (+ extensions)
                                                                                    ▼
published ◀──merit publish (≥ close_base+15 min)── final ◀──contiguity ok + final scores── closing
                                                                                    │
                                                                 provisional ◀──close_base+60 s (scorer)

live / closing / provisional / final / published ──void (Staff:exam.publish + reason)──▶ voided
```

`close_base` is the maximum effective `ends_at` over the exam's attempts: the exam-wide `ends_at` plus exam-wide extensions, or a later per-attempt extension. Every close phase is timed from it, never from the original `ends_at` (R-02).

| From | To | Trigger | Guard |
|---|---|---|---|
| draft | scheduled | `PATCH /v1/admin/exams/{id}` with a schedule | Blueprint or paper set; for live exams `result_publish_at ≥ ends_at + 15 min` and `solution_unlock_at ≥ result_publish_at` |
| scheduled | paper_frozen | `:freeze-paper` job at T−24 h | All `exam_papers` bundles generated and hash-checked; no unpublished versions in the paper |
| paper_frozen | live | Clock at `starts_at` (scheduler job) | — |
| live | closing | Clock at the effective `ends_at` (base plus exam-wide extensions) | — |
| closing | provisional | Scorer writes provisional `exam_results` at `close_base + 60 s` | Only when `provisional_score_enabled`; otherwise closing goes straight on to final |
| provisional or closing | final | Hard close at `close_base + 10 min`, then the SQS queue and DLQ are empty, then the contiguity `close_runs` row passes, then the final scores are written | A contiguity failure keeps the exam in its current state and opens an incident |
| final | published | `merit-snapshots/{id}:publish` | `now() ≥ result_publish_at`; snapshot status `draft` |
| published | final | Unpublish | Audited; the snapshot becomes `unpublished` |
| draft, scheduled, paper_frozen | cancelled | Admin cancel | Refunds through PAY (B6); registrations become `cancelled` |
| live, closing, provisional, final, published | voided | `POST /v1/admin/exams/{id}:void` (Staff:exam.publish) with a reason, e.g. a paper leak or a regional outage (R-21) | Audited. Unpublishes every merit snapshot, emits `exam.voided` (notifications through PLAN, fee refunds through PAY). Terminal state. |

An extension caps the cumulative extra time at `EXAM_MAX_EXTENSION_MIN` (default 60), so the exam token's `exp = ends_at + max extension + grace` never expires mid-exam. It also moves `result_publish_at` forward in the same transaction, keeping `ck_exams_publish_after_close` satisfied (PA-C6).

Non-live exams (mock, model_test, past_paper) go `draft → scheduled → live`. They stay `live` as an always-open window until `ends_at`, or with no end. Their attempts are scored at submit and never enter `closing`.

#### Attempt state machine (`exam_attempts.status`)

`not_started` (created at registration) → `in_progress` (`attempts:start`) → `submitted` (early manual submit marker, or an auto-end) → `closed` (hard close passed for this attempt) → `scored` (final result written). Non-live attempts go `in_progress → submitted → scored` synchronously. A takeover keeps the status, increments `epoch` and changes `device_session_id`.

#### `close_runs` phases

The phases run in order, with one row per `(exam_id, phase)`:

1. `provisional` (`close_base` + 60 s; also emits `exam.submitted` for auto-ended attempts)
2. `hard_close` (`close_base` + 10 min: reject further ingest; drain and wait until the SQS queue and the DLQ both report 0 visible and in-flight for 60 s)
3. `contiguity` (the gap check below)
4. `final` (final scoring)
5. `merit` (snapshot build)
6. `precompute` (Redis `exam:result:{attempt_id}`)

`status` is one of: `pending`, `running`, `passed`, `failed`. A rerun of a failed phase updates the same row and appends to `details.history`.

---

#### `exam.blueprints`

- **Why:** a reusable exam template: total marks, duration, negative marking, pass marks. Splits are stored per subject × syllabus year, because the SSC 2027+ CQ/short/MCQ/practical splits change by subject and year (market-validation §2.1).
- **Owner:** EXAM. The screen is the CMS-06 builder.
- **Features:** EXAM-01, EXAM-02, CMS-06, INST-04, INST-06.
- **Relations:**
  - N:1 `curriculum.tracks`, `curriculum.syllabus_versions` and `curriculum.taxonomy_nodes` (`subject_node_id`).
  - 1:N `blueprint_sections`, 1:N `exams`.
- **Read/write:** rare writes (content staff, at most tens a day). Reads come from mock generation and exam creation: Stage 1 under 1/s, Stage 4 about 20/s (cached).
- **Indexes:** `ix_blueprints_sv_subject (syllabus_version_id, subject_node_id, status)` serves "blueprints for this subject and year".
- **Risks:** none at scale. The risk is correctness: editing a blueprint after exams use it. **Mitigation:** blueprints are versioned. Editing an `active` blueprint creates a new row with `version + 1`, and exams keep their `blueprint_id`.
- **Build:** 5.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| track_id | uuid | no | — | FK `curriculum.tracks` RESTRICT |
| syllabus_version_id | uuid | no | — | FK `curriculum.syllabus_versions` RESTRICT |
| subject_node_id | uuid | no | — | FK `curriculum.taxonomy_nodes` RESTRICT |
| name | text | no | — | length 1–200 |
| version | int | no | 1 | uq `(track_id, syllabus_version_id, subject_node_id, name, version)` |
| total_marks | numeric(7,2) | no | — | CHECK > 0 |
| duration_min | int | no | — | CHECK 1–600 |
| pass_marks | numeric(7,2) | yes | — | CHECK ≤ total_marks |
| negative_mark_per_wrong | numeric(4,2) | no | 0 | CHECK 0–1 |
| calculator_allowed | bool | no | false | — |
| status | text | no | 'draft' | CHECK IN (draft, active, retired) |
| created_by | uuid | no | — | FK `identity.users` |
| created_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |
| updated_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |

- **Why:** section kinds are open-ended data, not an enum. Examples: CQ, short, MCQ, practical, financial_statement (accounting), descriptive (Bangla 1st paper). New kinds appear by subject and year.
- **Owner:** EXAM.
- **Features:** EXAM-01.
- **Relations:** 1:N `blueprint_sections`.
- **Read/write:** seeded; rarely changed; cached in process.
- **Indexes:** PK only.
- **Risks:** none.
- **Build:** 5.

| name_bn | text | no | — | — |

- **Why:** the sections of a blueprint. Each has a per-section timer, optional flag and an N-of-M choice: for example "answer 5 of 8" CQ, "10 of 15" short.

#### `exam.exams`

- **Why:** one exam instance with its schedule, audience and publication times. The five exam types share this table (EXAM-02).
- **Owner:** EXAM.
- **Features:** EXAM-02, EXAM-04, EXAM-04a, EXAM-06, EXAM-07, INST-04, OPS-01a.
- **Relations:**
  - N:1 `blueprints` (nullable: past-paper exams use `paper_id` → `qbank.papers`).
  - 1:N `exam_papers`, `registrations`, `exam_attempts`, `scoring_configs`, `merit_snapshots`, `close_runs`, `incidents`, `time_extensions`.
  - `fee_product_id` is a soft reference to `commerce.products`, checked through PAY. There is no FK: products are mutable and outside the allowed FK list.
- **Read/write:**
  - Writes come from staff.
  - Reads: the listing (Stage 1 about 20/s, Stage 4 about 300/s, edge-cached for 30 s), plus the status object, which is served from Redis `exam:status:{exam_id}` rather than this table.
- **Indexes:**
  - `ix_exams_listing (status, starts_at)` partial `WHERE audience_kind='public'` serves the listing.
  - `ix_exams_tenant (tenant_id, starts_at)` serves institution exams.
  - `ix_exams_kind (kind, status)`.
- **Risks:** a hot row on exam day (every status read). **Mitigation:** the status object lives in Redis and the row is read only by the control room and the scheduler.
- **Constraint:** `ck_exams_publish_after_close` is `CHECK (kind <> 'live' OR result_publish_at >= ends_at + interval '15 minutes')`. Extending an exam must move `result_publish_at` forward in the same transaction when needed.
- **Build:** 5.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| kind | text | no | — | CHECK IN (mock, model_test, live, past_paper, institution_batch) |
| blueprint_id | uuid | yes | — | FK `blueprints` RESTRICT |
| paper_id | uuid | yes | — | FK `qbank.papers` RESTRICT; CHECK `blueprint_id IS NOT NULL OR paper_id IS NOT NULL` |
| title_bn | text | no | — | — |
| title_en | text | no | — | — |
| tenant_id | uuid | yes | — | null = platform exam |
| audience_kind | text | no | 'public' | CHECK IN (public, restricted) |
| starts_at | timestamptz | yes | — | required for live and institution_batch |
| ends_at | timestamptz | yes | — | CHECK `ends_at > starts_at`; includes exam-wide extensions |
| late_entry_min | smallint | no | 15 | CHECK 0–60 |
| result_publish_at | timestamptz | yes | — | see `ck_exams_publish_after_close` |
| solution_unlock_at | timestamptz | yes | — | CHECK `solution_unlock_at >= result_publish_at` when both are set |
| provisional_score_enabled | bool | no | true | C3; false is recorded as an NFR-02 exception |
| registration_opens_at | timestamptz | yes | — | — |
| registration_closes_at | timestamptz | yes | — | CHECK ≤ starts_at |
| registration_cap | int | yes | — | launch cap 20,000 enforced by `runtime_config` (C17) |
| registered_count | int | no | 0 | CHECK `registered_count >= 0 AND (registration_cap IS NULL OR registered_count <= registration_cap)`. A seat is reserved with `UPDATE … SET registered_count = registered_count + 1 WHERE id = :id AND registered_count < registration_cap RETURNING`, with no advisory lock and no `count(*)` (R-20). A released reservation decrements it in the same transaction. |
| fee_product_id | uuid | yes | — | soft reference to `commerce.products` |
| settings | jsonb | no | '{}' | validated model: shuffle, sets, proctoring_required (01 §4.E G9-2) |
| status | text | no | 'draft' | CHECK IN (draft, scheduled, paper_frozen, live, closing, provisional, final, published, cancelled, voided) |

- **Why:** restricts an exam to institutions, batches or a user list. At P0 these are set up by ops (v2 §7.5); INST-04 makes it self-service.

#### `exam.exam_papers`

- **Why:** pre-generated, encrypted static bundles (AES-256-GCM, a per-exam key). They contain no answer keys, are generated at T−24 h and are stored in R2 under unguessable keys (NFR-01a).
- **Owner:** EXAM.
- **Features:** NFR-01a, EXAM-04, EXAM-09 (more `set_code`s in B8).
- **Relations:** N:1 `exams`; 1:N `exam_paper_items`.
- **Read/write:** written once per language and set at freeze. Read by the edge-key upload and by clients through a signed CDN URL; the database is never read at download time.
- **Indexes:** uq `(exam_id, lang, set_code)`.
- **Risks:** key leakage. **Mitigation:**
  - `key_ciphertext` is the data key encrypted by KMS. It is decrypted only by the job that pushes the key to the edge at T−5 min.
  - Never returned by any API.
- **Build:** 5.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| exam_id | uuid | no | — | FK `exams` RESTRICT |
| lang | text | no | — | CHECK IN (bn, en) |
| set_code | text | no | 'A' | CHECK `set_code ~ '^[A-D]$'`; only A at P0 (C16) |
| bundle_key | text | no | — | R2 object key, 128-bit random path |
| bundle_sha256 | bytea | no | — | length 32 |
| bytes | int | no | — | CHECK > 0 |
| key_ciphertext | bytea | no | — | KMS-wrapped |
| generated_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |

#### `exam.exam_paper_items`

- **Why:** pins the question versions in each bundle, so scoring uses exactly what was shipped.
- **Owner:** EXAM.
- **Features:** EXAM-01, EXAM-07.
- **Relations:** N:1 `exam_papers`, N:1 `blueprint_sections`, N:1 `qbank.question_versions`.
- **Read/write:** written at freeze (about 100 rows per bundle); read by the scorer (cached per exam in consumer memory).
- **Indexes:** PK `(exam_paper_id, position)`; `ix_epi_qv (question_version_id)` serves rescoring after a correction ("which exams used this version").
- **Risks:** none.
- **Build:** 5.

#### `exam.registrations`

- **Why:** registration happens before the start. It creates the attempt and the exam session token, so nothing is created at T0 (v2 §6.2 step 3).
- **Owner:** EXAM.
- **Features:** EXAM-04, PAY (paid exams), PLAN (reminders).
- **Relations:**
  - N:1 `exams` and `identity.users`; `track_enrolment_id` is a soft reference to `identity.track_enrolments` (FK allowed because users and enrolments are never deleted, but kept soft for consistency with other modules).
  - `order_id` is a soft reference to `commerce.orders` (B6).
  - 1:1 the ranked `exam_attempts` row.
- **Read/write:**
  - Registration bursts: Stage 1, 20,000 over the registration window (peaks about 50/s after a push).
  - 200k: about 500/s peak.
  - Read at the start and by the lock registry.
- **Indexes:**
  - uq `(exam_id, user_id)`.
  - `ix_registrations_user (user_id, registered_at DESC)` serves "my exams".
  - `ix_registrations_exam_status (exam_id, status)` serves counts and the in-memory lock registry for the fail-closed tutor lock.
- **Risks:** a registration cap race. **Mitigation:**
  - The conditional counter on `exams.registered_count`, decremented in the same transaction as a release or cancel, plus a nightly reconcile against `count(*) WHERE status IN ('reserved','confirmed')`.
  - Paid exams reserve the seat **before** payment, holding it 30 min. A capture after a lapse or on a full exam enqueues `pay.auto_refund` with a reconciliation note (R-20).
- **State machine:** `reserved` (paid; `reserved_until`) → `confirmed` on `payment.captured`; `reserved` → `released` by the `exam.release_reservations` job at lapse. A free exam starts `confirmed`. `confirmed` → `cancelled` (exam cancelled or voided, refund through PAY).
- **Build:** 5.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| exam_id | uuid | no | — | FK `exams` RESTRICT |
| user_id | uuid | no | — | FK `identity.users` RESTRICT |
| track_enrolment_id | uuid | yes | — | soft reference |
| order_id | uuid | yes | — | soft reference (B6) |
| district_id, institution_id, group_code, version_lang | smallint, uuid, text, text | yes | — | frozen scope at registration (PA-C1) |
| status | text | no | 'confirmed' | CHECK IN (reserved, confirmed, released, cancelled); ix `(status, reserved_until) WHERE status = 'reserved'` |
| reserved_until | timestamptz | yes | — | required when `status = 'reserved'` |
| registered_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |

#### `exam.exam_attempts`

- **Why:** one attempt per candidate. `attempt_no = 1` is the ranked attempt; re-attempts count for practice only (EXAM-08). It holds the device binding and epoch for the single-session rule (AUTH-02).
- **Owner:** EXAM.
- **Features:** EXAM-03, EXAM-04a, EXAM-05, EXAM-07, EXAM-08, AUTH-02.
- **Relations:**
  - N:1 `exams`, `registrations`, `identity.users`; soft reference to `identity.device_sessions`.
  - Soft 1:N to `exam_answer_batches` and `exam_answers_latest`, which are partitioned, so there is no FK.
  - 1:1 `exam_results`.
- **Read/write:**
  - Starts: Stage 1 up to 20,000 in the 15-minute lobby, plus late entry (about 25/s, peaks about 200/s at T0).
  - 200k: up to 2,000/s at T0. **Mitigation:** the row already exists from registration; start is a single-row update plus Redis writes.
  - `max_acked_seq` is the highest `acked_through` carried in any delivered SQS message, set by consumers with `GREATEST`. It is **advisory**: one input to the contiguity evidence and to monitoring. It is never the sole proof, because consumers wrote it.
- **Indexes:**
  - uq `(exam_id, user_id, attempt_no)`.
  - `ix_attempts_exam_status (exam_id, status)` serves the close runs and the participant count.
  - `ix_attempts_user (user_id, started_at DESC)` serves exam history.
- **Risks:** a T0 update storm. **Mitigation:**
  - The start endpoint is idempotent.
  - The row lock is per attempt, so there is no shared hot row.
  - Rehearsal measures it (B5 load test).
- **Build:** 5.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| exam_id | uuid | no | — | FK `exams` RESTRICT |
| user_id | uuid | no | — | FK `identity.users` RESTRICT |
| registration_id | uuid | yes | — | FK `registrations` RESTRICT (null for open mocks) |
| device_session_id | uuid | yes | — | soft reference |
| epoch | int | no | 0 | CHECK ≥ 0; incremented on takeover |
| attempt_no | smallint | no | 1 | CHECK ≥ 1 |
| kind | text | no | 'ranked' | CHECK IN (ranked, reattempt, practice); `ranked` only when attempt_no = 1 |
| exam_starts_on | date | no | — | copied from the exam (Dhaka date); partition key for the answer tables |
| started_at | timestamptz | yes | — | server time |
| ends_at | timestamptz | no | — | personal deadline including extensions |
| submitted_at | timestamptz | yes | — | — |
| submit_kind | text | yes | — | CHECK IN (manual, auto_end) |
| max_acked_seq | int | no | 0 | CHECK ≥ 0; highest batch `acked_through` seen by consumers; advisory only |
| status | text | no | 'not_started' | CHECK IN (not_started, in_progress, submitted, closed, scored) |
| created_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |

#### `exam.exam_answer_batches` (partitioned)

- **Why:** the append-only log of every acknowledged batch. It is the evidence for "zero data loss" and the input to the contiguity check (C1, v2 §6.3).
- **Owner:** EXAM. Written only by `exam-consumers`.
- **Features:** EXAM-05, NFR-03, NFR-01a.
- **Relations:** soft N:1 to `exam_attempts`. There is no FK, because the table is partitioned and written at high volume.
- **Read/write:**
  - Write once per acknowledged batch. Stage 1 (20k): about 560/s expected, 1,333/s ceiling.
  - 200k: about 5,600/s expected, 13,333/s ceiling, about 20M rows per exam. Consumers insert 500 per transaction (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`).
  - Reads only for the contiguity check and investigations.
- **Partitioning:** RANGE `(exam_starts_on)` monthly. PK `(attempt_id, seq, exam_starts_on)`. Partitions are archived to S3 Parquet 12 months after the merit list is final, then dropped (01 §5.6).
- **Indexes:**
  - PK serves the dedupe and the per-attempt scan.
  - `ix_eab_exam (exam_id, attempt_id)` is a local index per partition and serves the contiguity check per exam.
- **Meaning of `seq` (R-01).** `seq` is the per-attempt **batch** sequence number, contiguous from 1. Answers inside a batch have no seq of their own. Ingest acknowledges batch N only when N = acked + 1, re-acks N ≤ acked idempotently, and rejects N > acked + 1 with `seq_gap`.
- **Acknowledgement evidence.** The proof of what was acknowledged comes from sources the consumers do not control:
  - **Exam-Redis ledger:** `exam:acks:{attempt}`, written by ingest after each SQS success.
  - **Delivered messages:** every SQS message carries the cumulative `acked_through`, and consumers keep the highest in `exam_attempts.max_acked_seq`.
  - **Submit marker:** its `acked_through`.
  - **Client report:** the `acked_through` from status polls and the final flush (`exam:client_ack:{attempt}`).
  - **Aggregate counter:** `exam:sent:{exam_id}`, incremented by ingest after each new SQS success.
- **Contiguity check** (phase `contiguity`). The close runner loads, per attempt, `A = max(ledger, max_acked_seq, marker, client)` into a temporary table `acked(attempt_id, a)`. Both queries below must return zero rows:

```sql
-- 1. per attempt: the log must hold every batch seq 1..A
SELECT k.attempt_id, k.a, coalesce(l.n, 0) AS n, l.min_seq, l.max_seq
FROM acked k
LEFT JOIN (SELECT attempt_id, count(*) n, min(seq) min_seq, max(seq) max_seq
           FROM exam.exam_answer_batches
           WHERE exam_id = :exam_id AND exam_starts_on = :exam_starts_on
           GROUP BY attempt_id) l ON l.attempt_id = k.attempt_id
WHERE coalesce(l.max_seq, 0) < k.a                 -- acknowledged batch never logged (incl. a lost final batch)
   OR coalesce(l.n, 0) <> coalesce(l.max_seq, 0)   -- internal gap
   OR coalesce(l.min_seq, 1) <> 1;
-- 2. aggregate: distinct logged batches must equal ingest's sent counter
SELECT 1 WHERE (SELECT count(*) FROM exam.exam_answer_batches
                WHERE exam_id = :exam_id AND exam_starts_on = :exam_starts_on) <> :exam_sent_counter;
```

  Because `(attempt_id, seq)` is unique (the PK), `count = max(seq)` with `min(seq) = 1` means the log has no internal gaps, and `max(seq) ≥ A` means no acknowledged batch is missing. Any failure blocks publication and opens an incident.

  If the exam-Redis ledger was lost, the check runs on the message, marker and client evidence alone, and the incident log records that the evidence was degraded. When the ledger is missing, ingest accepts the client's `acked_through` once with a `resync` flag rather than forcing a resend from seq 1. A recovery resend may be split into consecutive batches of at most 200 answers.
- **Risks:**
  - Write saturation at 200k (about 19k row upserts/s combined with the latest table; architecture review §4.4). **Mitigation:** batched inserts; a dedicated instance option (01 §5.9); rehearsal sizing.
  - Large partitions. **Mitigation:** monthly partitions, with local indexes only.
- **Build:** 5.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| attempt_id | uuid | no | — | PK part |
| seq | int | no | — | PK part; CHECK ≥ 1 |
| exam_starts_on | date | no | — | PK part; partition key |
| exam_id | uuid | no | — | — |
| received_at | timestamptz | no | — | ingest receipt time (server); used in the tie-break |
| answers | jsonb | no | — | array of `{question_version_id, response, device_effective_at, change_no}`; `change_no` is a per-question counter incremented on every change; there is no per-answer seq |
| acked_through | int | no | — | cumulative ack carried in the SQS message (= `seq` for a normal send; the client claim for a `resync`) |
| is_resync | bool | no | false | ingest accepted a client claim after exam-Redis ledger loss |
| ingest_node | text | no | — | ingest instance id |
| is_submit_marker | bool | no | false | the early manual submit travels through the same queue and carries the client's final `acked_through` |

#### `exam.exam_answers_latest` (partitioned)

- **Why:** the latest answer per question for scoring. The highest batch `seq` wins, and within one batch the highest `change_no` wins: the guard is `(excluded.seq, excluded.change_no) > (l.seq, l.change_no)`, and a `change_no smallint` column sits beside `seq`.
- **Owner:** EXAM (`exam-consumers`).
- **Features:** EXAM-05, EXAM-07.
- **Relations:** soft N:1 `exam_attempts`; N:1 `qbank.question_versions` (no FK; hot path).
- **Read/write:**
  - An upsert for each answer in each batch. Stage 1 up to about 1,900 rows/s; 200k up to about 19k/s at the ceiling, about 20M rows (100 per candidate).
  - Read by the scorer once per attempt and by recovery (`/acknowledged`).
- **Upsert rule:**

```sql
INSERT INTO exam.exam_answers_latest AS l
  (attempt_id, question_version_id, exam_starts_on, seq, change_no, response, device_effective_at, received_at, is_late)
VALUES (...)
ON CONFLICT (attempt_id, question_version_id, exam_starts_on)
DO UPDATE SET seq = excluded.seq, change_no = excluded.change_no, response = excluded.response,
              device_effective_at = excluded.device_effective_at,
              received_at = excluded.received_at, is_late = excluded.is_late
WHERE (excluded.seq, excluded.change_no) > (l.seq, l.change_no);
```

  Out-of-order and duplicate deliveries therefore cannot overwrite a newer answer. A question appears at most once per batch (ingest keeps only its highest `change_no`), so ties on both columns are impossible.
- **Partitioning:** same as `exam_answer_batches`. PK `(attempt_id, question_version_id, exam_starts_on)`.
- **Indexes:** the PK serves the scorer scan by attempt (a prefix of the PK).
- **Risks:** HOT-update bloat. **Mitigation:** `fillfactor = 80`; partitions are short-lived.
- **Build:** 5.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| attempt_id | uuid | no | — | PK part |
| question_version_id | uuid | no | — | PK part |
| exam_starts_on | date | no | — | PK part; partition key |
| seq | int | no | — | CHECK ≥ 1; batch that carried this answer |
| change_no | smallint | no | 1 | CHECK ≥ 1; per-question change counter from the device |
| response | jsonb | no | — | validated per question type |
| device_effective_at | timestamptz | yes | — | client effective time |
| received_at | timestamptz | no | — | server receipt time |
| is_late | bool | no | false | `device_effective_at > ends_at`, or received after the hard close; stored but not auto-scored (v2 §6.3) |

- **Features:** OPS-01a, CMS-04a, EXAM-06.
- **Relations:** N:1 `exams`; referenced by `exam_results.scoring_config_version` and `merit_snapshots.scoring_config_version`.
- **Read/write:** a few rows per exam.
- **Indexes:** uq `(exam_id, version)`.
- **Risks:** none.
- **Build:** 5.

#### `exam.exam_results`

- **Why:** the score per attempt, provisional (no rank, labelled) and then final. Rank lives in `merit_entries`. Holds the tie-break inputs.
- **Owner:** EXAM.
- **Features:** EXAM-07, NFR-02 (provisional in 5 s), ANL-02.
- **Relations:** 1:1 `exam_attempts` (PK = attempt_id); N:1 `exams`.
- **Read/write:**
  - Writes come from the scorer: 200k attempts in about 12.5 s (v2 §6.1), in upsert chunks of 1,000.
  - Reads come from the result page through Redis precompute (no database query at publish time) and from exam history (ANL-02).
- **Indexes:**
  - `ix_results_exam_rank (exam_id, phase, score DESC, wrong ASC, time_used_ms ASC, last_answer_received_at ASC)` serves the merit sort.
  - `ix_results_user (user_id, computed_at DESC)` serves exam history.
- **Risks:** the final pass overwrites the provisional row. That is intended: provisional is not a record, and the final row is the record. A rescore writes a new `scoring_config_version`; the previous scores survive in the merit snapshots.
- **Build:** 5.

#### `exam.merit_snapshots`

- **Why:** versioned, immutable merit lists. Ops can publish, unpublish and export them (EXAM-06). A rescore creates a new version; republication needs ops approval (v2 §7.1 step 10).
- **Owner:** EXAM.
- **Features:** EXAM-06, CMS-04a, EXAM-12 (certificates read the published snapshot).
- **Relations:** N:1 `exams`; 1:N `merit_entries`.
- **Read/write:** a few rows per exam; built at end + 15 min or later.
- **Indexes:** uq `(exam_id, version)`; partial uq `(exam_id) WHERE status='published'` (one published snapshot per exam).
- **Immutability:**
  - The status changes `draft → published → unpublished | superseded`.
  - Entries are never updated after the snapshot commits.
  - The app role has no `UPDATE` on `merit_entries`.
  - A correction creates version + 1.
- **Risks:** none.
- **Build:** 5.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |

| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| exam_id | uuid | no | — | FK `exams` RESTRICT |

| published_by | uuid | yes | — | FK `identity.users` |
| export_key | text | yes | — | S3 key of the CSV and PDF export |

#### `exam.merit_entries`

- **Why:** national, district, institution and group ranks for each attempt in a snapshot.
- **Owner:** EXAM.
- **Features:** EXAM-06, EXAM-07, ANL-02, NFR-07 (the public list exposes only first name and institution).
- **Relations:** N:1 `merit_snapshots` (CASCADE only while in draft); 1:1 `exam_attempts` per snapshot.
- **Read/write:**
  - Bulk `COPY` of up to 200k rows per snapshot (under 2 minutes including all scopes; v2 §6.1).
  - Reads: the merit list page, at the top N per scope, cached.
- **Tie-break (rank order):**
  1. Higher `score`.
  2. Fewer `wrong`.
  3. Less `time_used_ms`.
  4. Earlier `last_answer_received_at`.

  All times are from server receipt, never from the device. Exact ties after all four share a rank (standard competition ranking, 1-2-2-4). Ranks are computed with `RANK() OVER (PARTITION BY scope ORDER BY …)` in one REPEATABLE READ transaction.
- **Indexes:**
  - PK `(snapshot_id, attempt_id)`.
  - `ix_merit_national (snapshot_id, national_rank)`, `ix_merit_district (snapshot_id, district_id, district_rank)`, `ix_merit_institution (snapshot_id, institution_id, institution_rank)` serve the scoped lists. These need the proposed scope columns; see "Proposed additions".
  - `ix_merit_user (user_id)` serves rank history.
- **Risks:** about 200k rows per snapshot and version. **Mitigation:** superseded snapshots older than 12 months are archived to S3.
- **Build:** 5.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| snapshot_id | uuid | no | — | PK part; FK `merit_snapshots` RESTRICT |
| attempt_id | uuid | no | — | PK part |
| user_id | uuid | no | — | FK `identity.users` |
| national_rank | int | no | — | CHECK ≥ 1 |
| district_rank | int | yes | — | — |
| institution_rank | int | yes | — | — |
| group_rank | int | yes | — | — |
| percentile | numeric(5,2) | no | — | CHECK 0–100 |
| institution_label | text | no | 'self_reported' | CHECK IN (self_reported, verified) (C15) |

#### `exam.time_extensions`

- **Why:** "Extend time" for a whole exam or one attempt. `ends_at` reaches clients in the next acknowledgement and through the 60 s status poll (v2 §6.2 step 12).
- **Owner:** EXAM.
- **Features:** OPS-01a, EXAM-04a.
- **Relations:** N:1 `exams`; N:1 `exam_attempts` (nullable = exam-wide).
- **Read/write:** rare. Applying an extension updates `exams.ends_at` or `exam_attempts.ends_at` and Redis `exam:status:{exam_id}` in the same transaction plus a post-commit Redis write. The status is rebuildable from this table.
- **Indexes:** `ix_time_ext_exam (exam_id, created_at)`.
- **Risks:** an extension during the late window. **Mitigation:** extensions are refused once `hard_close` has started.
- **Build:** 5.

#### `exam.late_answer_cases`

- **Why:** answers whose device effective time is after `ends_at`, or that arrive after the hard close. They are stored and flagged, never auto-scored, and decided in the control room using the device sequence log (v2 §6.3).
- **Owner:** EXAM.
- **Features:** EXAM-05, OPS-01a.
- **Relations:** N:1 `exam_attempts`.
- **Read/write:** expected under 2% of candidates. Written by consumers.
- **Indexes:** `ix_late_attempt (attempt_id)`; `ix_late_undecided (decision) WHERE decision = 'pending'`.
- **Risks:** none.
- **Build:** 5.

#### `exam.integrity_events` (partitioned)

- **Why:** device-side anti-cheat logs: app switch, tab blur, screenshot attempts (EXAM-09a). Logs only; no automatic penalties.
- **Owner:** EXAM.
- **Features:** EXAM-09a, EXAM-09b.
- **Relations:** soft N:1 `exam_attempts`.
- **Read/write:** batched by clients. 200k: about 2 events per candidate, mostly in bursts at the end.
- **Partitioning:** RANGE `created_on` monthly; PK `(id, created_on)`; retained 12 months.
- **Indexes:** `ix_integrity_attempt (attempt_id, occurred_at)`.
- **Risks:** volume spikes. **Mitigation:** the client aggregates `count` per kind per minute.
- **Build:** 8.

---

### 4.15 `ai` (AI)

Shared notes for this schema:

- **Writers.**
  - `ai-gateway` writes threads, messages, citations, usage, photo solves and pending questions.
  - `workers` write marking, eval, summary and coach rows.
  - `api` reads canonical answers through the AI read interface, so cached explanations survive a gateway outage (v2 §4.5).
- **Provider calls.** A pooled connection or an open transaction is never held across a provider call (01 §5.1).
- **Rates.**
  - Stage 1 is about 3 tutor messages/s.
  - Stage 4 is about 300/s, with up to about 3,000 concurrent streams bounded by admission control (`ai:inflight:{user}`, provider tokens-per-minute).
- **Partition retention.**
  - `tutor_messages` and `tutor_citations`: monthly partitions, dropped at 13 months (the 12-month thread rule plus 1 month of grace).
  - `ai_usage`: dropped at 24 months, for cost audit.

#### `ai.tutor_threads`

- **Why:** one thread list per subject per enrolment; "new chat" creates a thread (AI-01). The model sees the last six turns plus a rolling `summary`.
- **Owner:** AI.
- **Features:** AI-01, AI-06 (mode), PAR-03 and teacher mode locks.
- **Relations:**
  - N:1 `identity.users`; N:1 `curriculum.taxonomy_nodes` (subject).
  - Soft reference `track_enrolment_id`.
  - Soft 1:N `tutor_messages` (partitioned, so no FK).
- **Read/write:** read at each message (one row); writes for `last_message_at` and the summary on every turn. Stage 4 about 300/s, one row per user, no hotspot.
- **Indexes:**
  - `ix_threads_user_subject (user_id, subject_node_id, last_message_at DESC) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` serves the thread list.
  - `ix_threads_expiry (expires_at) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL` serves the retention job.
- **Risks:** none.
- **Retention:** `expires_at = last_message_at + 12 months`. On student delete, `deleted_at` is set and a job erases the message text within 24 h.
- **Build:** 4 (mode columns used from 7).

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| user_id | uuid | no | — | FK `identity.users` RESTRICT |
| track_enrolment_id | uuid | yes | — | soft reference |
| subject_node_id | uuid | no | — | FK `curriculum.taxonomy_nodes` |
| title | text | yes | — | auto from the first message, moderated |
| mode | text | no | 'direct' | CHECK IN (direct, socratic) |
| mode_locked_by | text | yes | — | CHECK IN (parent, teacher); read through the PAR and INST interfaces |
| summary | text | yes | — | rolling summary (no PII beyond the thread) |
| summary_upto_at | timestamptz | yes | — | — |
| status | text | no | 'active' | CHECK IN (active, archived) |
| last_message_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |
| expires_at | timestamptz | no | — | — |
| deleted_at | timestamptz | yes | — | — |
| created_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |

#### `ai.tutor_messages` (partitioned)

- **Why:** user and assistant messages, with routing, cache and verification metadata. The row is created **before** the provider call (status `pending`) and completed afterwards (v2 §3.5).
- **Owner:** AI.
- **Features:** AI-01, AI-02, AI-04, AI-05, AI-05a, AI-15.
- **Relations:**
  - Soft N:1 `tutor_threads` and `identity.users`; 1:N `tutor_citations`; 1:N `feedback`.
  - Request idempotency goes through `platform.idempotency_keys` (key → message id), because a unique key on a partitioned table would need `created_on`.
- **Read/write:**
  - Two writes per turn (user and assistant rows), plus a completion update.
  - Reads: the thread history (cursor by `created_at`), and the last six turns for context.
  - Stage 1 about 6 rows/s; Stage 4 about 600/s.
- **Partitioning:** RANGE `created_on` monthly; PK `(id, created_on)`.
- **Indexes:**
  - `ix_msg_thread (thread_id, created_at)`, local, serves history and context.
  - `ix_msg_user_day (user_id, created_on)` serves support lookups.
- **Status values:** `pending`, `answered`, `not_sure`, `ocr_unclear`, `quota_exhausted`, `locked_exam`, `unavailable`, `refused` (safety). The final SSE event exposes v2 §3.2's six statuses; `refused` is shown as the fixed safe response.
- **Risks:** large text. **Mitigation:** text is capped at 8 KB per user message and 16 KB per assistant message; monthly partitions are dropped.
- **Build:** 4.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK part |
| created_on | date | no | — | PK part (UTC date of `created_at`) |
| thread_id | uuid | no | — | — |
| user_id | uuid | no | — | — |
| role | text | no | — | CHECK IN (user, assistant, system_note) |
| action | text | yes | — | CHECK IN (free_text, why_wrong, explain_simpler, example, explain_lang, shortcut, hint, photo_solve) |
| context | jsonb | no | '{}' | `{answer_id, question_version_id, option_label, chapter_node_id}` |
| text | text | yes | — | null after deletion |
| status | text | no | 'pending' | see above |
| model | text | yes | — | — |
| model_tier | text | yes | — | CHECK IN (cheap, strong, top) |
| prompt_version | text | yes | — | soft reference `prompt_versions.id` |
| cache_result | text | yes | — | CHECK IN (hit, miss, none) |
| verify_result | jsonb | yes | — | `{citations_ok, key_agrees, math_ok, moderation_ok, retries}` |
| latency_ms | int | yes | — | first-token and total in `verify_result.timing` |
| created_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |
| completed_at | timestamptz | yes | — | — |

#### `ai.tutor_citations` (partitioned)

- **Why:** the citations on each answer. Page numbers and ids are built only from `content_chunks` and `qbank` metadata, never parsed from model text (AI-02a).
- **Owner:** AI.
- **Features:** AI-02a.
- **Relations:** soft N:1 `tutor_messages`; soft N:1 `content_chunks`, `content_sources`, `qbank.question_versions`, `curriculum.taxonomy_nodes`.
- **Read/write:** 1–4 rows per answered message, read with the message.
- **Partitioning:** RANGE `created_on` monthly, aligned with the messages; PK `(message_id, ordinal, created_on)`.
- **Indexes:** PK serves the lookup by message.
- **Verification rule:** `verified = true` only if all three hold:
  - the cited chunk or version is in the retrieved, permission-filtered set for this request;
  - it exists and is published;
  - the quote is a span of the chunk text.

  An unverified citation is never shown as fact.
- **Risks:** none.
- **Build:** 4.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| message_id | uuid | no | — | PK part |
| ordinal | smallint | no | — | PK part; CHECK ≥ 1 |
| created_on | date | no | — | PK part |
| source_type | text | no | — | CHECK IN (textbook_page, question_version, explanation, concept_note) |
| chunk_id | uuid | yes | — | — |
| source_id | uuid | yes | — | — |
| chapter_node_id | uuid | yes | — | — |
| page_no | int | yes | — | copied from chunk metadata only |
| question_version_id | uuid | yes | — | — |
| quote | text | yes | — | ≤ 500 chars |
| verified | bool | no | — | — |

#### `ai.ai_usage` (partitioned)

- **Why:** one row per provider call, for per-user cost, routing ceilings, budgets and BI (AI-15, OPS-06). Cost is in **micro-USD** (`bigint`), so there is no float.
- **Owner:** AI.
- **Features:** AI-15, AI-05a, OPS-06.
- **Relations:** soft N:1 `identity.users`; soft reference to `tutor_messages`, or a `job_ref` for marking, OCR, embeddings and evals.
- **Read/write:**
  - Write once per call: Stage 1 about 4/s, Stage 4 about 400/s.
  - Reads: the per-user daily sum. The fast path is Redis; this table is the record, reconciled nightly.
- **Partitioning:** RANGE `created_on` monthly; PK `(id, created_on)`; 24-month retention.
- **Indexes:** `ix_usage_user_day (user_id, dhaka_date)` serves the per-user ceiling of US$0.15 a day; `ix_usage_route_day (route, dhaka_date)` serves cost per route.
- **Quota-charge-once rule:**
  - The user-visible quota (messages and photos per Dhaka day) is charged once per `message_id`, only when the message reaches `answered` (or a final `not_sure` with content). The charge uses Redis `SETNX quota_charged:{message_id}` plus `INCR quota:{user}:{feature}:{day}`.
  - Retries, cache hits on a retry, `ocr_unclear`, `unavailable` and `locked_exam` are never charged.
  - `ai_usage` records provider cost for every call regardless of quota.
  - A nightly job recomputes the quota counters from the messages and fixes drift.
- **Risks:** none.
- **Build:** 4.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK part |
| created_on | date | no | — | PK part |
| user_id | uuid | yes | — | null for system jobs |
| route | text | no | — | CHECK IN (tutor_cheap, tutor_strong, tutor_top, photo_ocr, photo_solve, marking, content_ocr, embedding, eval, summary, coach, quiz, voice) |
| provider | text | no | — | — |
| model | text | no | — | — |
| tokens_in | int | no | 0 | CHECK ≥ 0 |
| tokens_out | int | no | 0 | CHECK ≥ 0 |
| tokens_cached | int | no | 0 | CHECK ≥ 0 |
| cost_micro_usd | bigint | no | 0 | CHECK ≥ 0 |
| message_id | uuid | yes | — | — |
| job_ref | text | yes | — | — |
| dhaka_date | date | no | — | — |
| created_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |

#### `ai.canonical_answers`

- **Why:** the shared explain cache.
  - **Key:** `(question_version_id, action, option_label, lang, prompt_version)`.
  - **C5 privacy rule:** it holds content ids only, and entries are generated **without any personal context** (no history, no profile beyond class and version, no thread).
  - Every student who picks option খ on this version shares the entry; a hit costs nothing and returns in under 500 ms.
  - It is stored in Postgres, so cached answers survive a gateway outage (v2 §4.5).
- **Owner:** AI.
- **Features:** AI-04, AI-15, NFR-03.
- **Relations:** N:1 `qbank.question_versions` (FK allowed). A correction of the version leaves the entry tied to the old version, and the new version gets new entries.
- **Read/write:**
  - Reads on every explain action: Stage 4 about 200/s, also cached in Redis by key.
  - Writes on a miss only.
  - Pre-generation for exams: 800 entries per 100-question paper (v2 §8.6).
- **Indexes:** uq `(question_version_id, action, option_label, lang, prompt_version) NULLS NOT DISTINCT` serves the cache lookup.
- **Risks:** a wrong cached text served to many students. **Mitigation:**
  - A thumbs-down creates `cms.ai_review_items`.
  - The correction endpoint sets `status = 'corrected'` and writes a replacement.
  - Release test: identical text for two users with different histories (v2 §4.8).
- **Build:** 4.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| question_version_id | uuid | no | — | FK `qbank.question_versions` RESTRICT |
| action | text | no | — | CHECK IN (why_wrong, explain_simpler, example, explain_lang, shortcut) |
| option_label | text | yes | — | for why_wrong |
| lang | text | no | — | CHECK IN (bn, en) |
| prompt_version | text | no | — | — |
| text | text | no | — | — |
| citations | jsonb | no | '[]' | the same shape as `tutor_citations`, verified at generation |
| status | text | no | 'active' | CHECK IN (active, corrected, retired) |
| model | text | no | — | — |
| corrected_by | uuid | yes | — | FK `identity.users` |
| created_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |

#### `ai.content_sources`

- **Why:** retrieval sources (NCTB textbooks, the verified-explanation corpus, notes) with licence status. Sources with `licence_status <> 'licensed'` are never retrieved or cited (D1).
- **Owner:** AI.
- **Features:** AI-02, AI-02a.
- **Relations:** N:1 `curriculum.syllabus_versions`, `taxonomy_nodes`; 1:N `content_chunks`.
- **Read/write:** low.
- **Indexes:** `ix_sources_sv (syllabus_version_id, subject_node_id, licence_status)`.
- **Risks:** legal. **Mitigation:** a licence gate in the retrieval filter.
- **Build:** 4.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| kind | text | no | — | CHECK IN (nctb_textbook, explanation_corpus, concept_note, other_licensed) |
| title | text | no | — | — |
| syllabus_version_id | uuid | yes | — | FK |
| subject_node_id | uuid | yes | — | FK |
| edition_year | smallint | yes | — | — |
| licence_status | text | no | 'pending' | CHECK IN (licensed, pending, refused, not_required) |

#### `ai.content_chunks`

- **Why:** retrieval chunks, carrying the page and chapter metadata that citations are built from.
- **Owner:** AI.
- **Features:** AI-02, AI-02a, AI-03.
- **Relations:** N:1 `content_sources`; N:1 `taxonomy_nodes` (chapter).
- **Read/write:**
  - Bulk-written at ingestion: about 15,000 pages at roughly 4 chunks each, so about 60k rows, plus the explanation corpus of about 300k rows.
  - Read on every retrieval (top 6 hybrid). Stage 4 about 300 queries/s, served on a read replica from Stage 2 (01 §5.9).
- **Indexes:**
  - HNSW `ix_chunks_embedding (embedding vector_cosine_ops)` with `m=16, ef_construction=64`.
  - GIN `ix_chunks_tsv (tsv)`.
  - `ix_chunks_filter (source_id, chapter_node_id, lang)`.
  - Filtering by `tenant_id` and licence happens before ranking, by joining the active sources.
- **Risks:**
  - HNSW recall under filters (chapter, syllabus version, tenant, `is_retrievable`). **Mitigation (R-23):** pgvector ≥ 0.8 iterative scan (`hnsw.iterative_scan = relaxed_order`), a tuned `ef_search`, partial indexes or exact search for tenant-private chunks (B8), and filtered queries in the retrieval eval and the 500-query gate.
  - Index memory. **Mitigation:** about 360k × 1024 × 4 B ≈ 1.5 GB; sized in the instance plan.
- **Build:** 4.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| source_id | uuid | no | — | FK `content_sources` RESTRICT |
| chapter_node_id | uuid | yes | — | FK `curriculum.taxonomy_nodes` |
| page_no | int | yes | — | — |
| ordinal | int | no | — | uq `(source_id, ordinal)` |
| lang | text | no | — | CHECK IN (bn, en) |
| text | text | no | — | — |
| tsv | tsvector | no | — | `simple` configuration over normalised text |
| embedding | vector(1024) | yes | — | bge-m3 |
| tenant_id | uuid | yes | — | null = platform |
| token_count | int | no | — | — |

#### `ai.photo_solves`

- **Risks:** images of people. **Mitigation:** rejected and deleted at moderation; never cached.
- **Build:** 4.

#### `ai.marking_jobs`

- **Why:** the drafting runs for one submission. It repeats runs, measures disagreement and computes triage priority for the teacher queue (v2 §4.10, the triage step). If drafting fails or times out, the submission goes to the queue unmarked.
- **Owner:** AI.
- **Features:** AI-07, AI-07c.
- **Relations:** 1:1 `institution.submissions` (soft reference; INST owns submissions).
- **Read/write:** one per submission. Pilot about 1,000 a day.
- **Indexes:** uq `submission_id`; `ix_marking_status (status, started_at)`.
- **Risks:** cost (US$0.058 per run on the top model). **Mitigation:** the run count comes from the accuracy test; a separate marking quota (D2).
- **Build:** 6.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| submission_id | uuid | no | — | uq |
| status | text | no | 'queued' | CHECK IN (queued, running, drafted, failed, timed_out) |
| runs | smallint | no | 0 | CHECK 0–5 |
| disagreement | numeric(5,2) | yes | — | max part-mark spread across runs |
| triage_priority | smallint | no | 0 | higher = earlier in the queue |
| triage_reasons | text[] | no | '{}' | values: disagreement, unreadable, injection_flag, boundary_mark, failed |
| started_at | timestamptz | yes | — | — |
| finished_at | timestamptz | yes | — | — |

#### `ai.gradings`

- **Why:** AI drafts and teacher marks for a submission. Rows are **never overwritten**:
  - Each AI run is a row with `source = 'ai_draft'`.
  - The teacher's confirmation or change is a new row with `source = 'teacher'`, `is_final = true`, `supersedes_id` → the draft.
  - Drafts stay for audit and for the accuracy statistics.
- **Owner:** AI.
- **Features:** AI-07, AI-07b, AI-07c, EXAM-10, INST-08.
- **Relations:**
  - Polymorphic soft reference `(submission_ref_type, submission_ref_id)` → `institution.submissions` or `exam.written_answers`.
  - 1:N `grading_parts`.
- **Read/write:** 1–3 draft rows plus 1 teacher row per submission. The teacher queue reads drafts.
- **Indexes:**
  - `ix_gradings_ref (submission_ref_type, submission_ref_id, created_at)`.
  - Partial uq `(submission_ref_type, submission_ref_id) WHERE is_final` (one final mark per submission). The index is not deferrable, so a re-mark must `UPDATE … SET is_final = false` on the old final **before** inserting the new final row, in the same transaction (R-19).
- **Risks:** none.
- **Access (R-03):** written only by AI. INST and EXAM create work through `ai.request_marking(MarkingRequest)` and read through `ai.get_drafts`. AI never reads INST or EXAM tables.
- **Build:** 6.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| submission_ref_type | text | no | — | CHECK IN (inst_submission, exam_written) |
| submission_ref_id | uuid | no | — | — |
| source | text | no | — | CHECK IN (ai_draft, teacher) |
| run_no | smallint | yes | — | for ai_draft |
| model | text | yes | — | required for ai_draft |
| prompt_version | text | yes | — | required for ai_draft |
| total_marks | numeric(7,2) | no | — | CHECK ≥ 0 |
| created_by | uuid | yes | — | teacher user id for source teacher; CHECK `source <> 'teacher' OR created_by IS NOT NULL` |
| supersedes_id | uuid | yes | — | FK `gradings` |
| is_final | bool | no | false | CHECK `NOT is_final OR source = 'teacher'` |
| created_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |

#### `ai.accuracy_reports`

- **Why:** published marking accuracy per subject and part, measured against two teachers (AI-07d, v2 §4.10). A model or prompt change needs a new report before release. The gate column decides what ships:
  - `teacher_review`: QWK ≥ 0.70, no more than 0.10 below teacher-to-teacher agreement, bias ≤ 0.15 SD.
  - `practice_estimate`: QWK ≥ 0.8.
  - `failed`: below the first row.
- **Owner:** AI.
- **Features:** AI-07d.
- **Relations:** N:1 `curriculum.taxonomy_nodes`.
- **Read/write:** low; the public read is cached.
- **Indexes:** `ix_accuracy_subject (subject_node_id, part_label, measured_on DESC)`; partial uq on the published report per `(subject_node_id, part_label, model, prompt_version)`.
- **Risks:** none.
- **Build:** 6.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| subject_node_id | uuid | no | — | FK |
| part_label | text | yes | — | null = total |
| model | text | no | — | — |
| prompt_version | text | no | — | — |
| sample_size | int | no | — | CHECK ≥ 400 for a published gate |
| metrics | jsonb | no | — | `{qwk, qwk_teacher_teacher, exact, within_one, mae, bias_sd, large_errors, to_teacher_share}` |
| interval | jsonb | no | — | bootstrap CI resampling students and questions |
| gate | text | no | — | CHECK IN (teacher_review, practice_estimate, failed) |
| measured_on | date | no | — | — |
| published_at | timestamptz | yes | — | — |

- **Build:** 9.

| kind | text | no | — | CHECK IN (bcs_viva, bup_interview, afmc_interview, other) |
| rubric | jsonb | no | — | — |
| status | text | no | 'draft' | CHECK IN (draft, active, retired) |

---

### 4.16 `institution` (INST)

#### Tenancy and row-level security plan

| Stage | Tenant | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| **P0 (Build 6)** | Each teacher batch is its own tenant: `batches.tenant_id = batches.id`, set at creation | Every batch-scoped row (`written_tasks`, `submissions`) copies `tenant_id`. Services filter by the actor's batch membership (`can(actor, 'batch.read', batch)`), and repository functions require a `tenant_id` argument. No RLS policies yet. |
| **P1 (Build 8)** | A workspace is the tenant: `workspaces.id = tenant_id`. Batches created inside a workspace take `tenant_id = workspace_id`. Standalone P0 batches keep their own id as tenant. | RLS is enabled on tenant-bearing tables in `institution`, on `qbank.questions`, `qbank.question_versions`, `exam.exams`, `ai.content_chunks` and `platform.search_documents`. Policy: `USING (tenant_id IS NULL OR tenant_id = current_setting('app.tenant_id', true)::uuid OR current_setting('app.bypass_rls', true) = 'on')`. `SET LOCAL app.tenant_id` is set inside each transaction (it works under PgBouncer transaction mode). `bypass_rls` is available only to the audited break-glass role (v2 §7.3). |
| **Tests (8)** | — | Automated cross-tenant read attempts on every release, covering every endpoint that takes a tenant-scoped id (v2 §8.5). |

The access rules follow v2 §4.10:

- A teacher reads only their own batches' submissions.
- A student reads only their own submissions.
- Leaving a batch stops access to new work.
- Ops access is audited.

#### `institution.teacher_approvals`

- **Why:** ops approves teacher accounts before they can create batches (INST-02a).
- **Owner:** INST.
- **Features:** INST-02a.
- **Relations:** N:1 `identity.users`. An approval grants `identity.role_grants(role='teacher')` through the AUTH interface.
- **Read/write:** low.
- **Indexes:** partial uq `(user_id) WHERE status IN ('pending','approved')`; `ix_ta_status (status, created_at)` serves the ops queue.
- **Risks:** fake teachers. **Mitigation:** evidence review; revocation removes the role.
- **Build:** 6.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| user_id | uuid | no | — | FK `identity.users` |
| status | text | no | 'pending' | CHECK IN (pending, approved, rejected, revoked) |
| subject_node_ids | uuid[] | no | '{}' | — |
| evidence | jsonb | no | '{}' | institution, phone or reference; files as keys |
| reviewed_by | uuid | yes | — | FK `identity.users` |
| reviewed_at | timestamptz | yes | — | — |
| created_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |

#### `institution.batches`

- **Why:** a teacher batch with a join code; the tenant at P0. A section within a workspace from B8 (`kind = 'section'`).
- **Owner:** INST.
- **Features:** INST-02a, INST-01, AUTH-09.
- **Relations:** N:1 `identity.users` (teacher); N:1 `workspaces` (B8, nullable); 1:N `batch_members`, `written_tasks`.
- **Read/write:** low writes; join-code lookups are rate-limited.
- **Indexes:** uq `join_code`; `ix_batches_teacher (teacher_user_id, status)`; `ix_batches_workspace (workspace_id)`.
- **Risks:** join-code guessing. **Mitigation:** 8-character codes from an unambiguous alphabet, expiry, T-write rate limits, rotation.
- **Build:** 6.

| Column | Type | Null | Default | Constraints |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| id | uuid | no | — | PK |
| tenant_id | uuid | no | — | = id at P0; = workspace_id for workspace batches |
| workspace_id | uuid | yes | — | FK `workspaces` (added in B8) |
| teacher_user_id | uuid | no | — | FK `identity.users` |
| kind | text | no | 'batch' | CHECK IN (batch, section) |
| name | text | no | — | moderated |
| subject_node_id | uuid | yes | — | FK `curriculum.taxonomy_nodes` |
| join_code | text | no | — | uq; CHECK `join_code ~ '^[A-HJ-NP-Z2-9]{8}$'` |
| join_code_expires_at | timestamptz | yes | — | — |
| status | text | no | 'active' | CHECK IN (active, archived) |
| created_at | timestamptz | no | now() | — |

#### `institution.batch_members`

- **Why:** the students and teachers in a batch. Joining by code requires guardian consent for under-18s (v2 §4.10).
- **Owner:** INST.
- **Features:** INST-02a, AUTH-09.
- **Relations:** N:M between `batches` and `identity.users`.

| user_id | uuid | no | — | PK part; FK `identity.users` |
| role | text | no | 'student' | CHECK IN (student, teacher) |
| status | text | no | 'active' | CHECK IN (active, left, removed) |

| left_at | timestamptz | yes | — | — |

#### `institution.submissions`

- **Why:** one student's answer to one task item. Its lifecycle: `uploading → submitted → drafting → in_review → teacher_checked → released`.
- **Owner:** INST.
- **Features:** AI-07, AI-07c, INST-02a.
- **Relations:**
  - N:1 `written_tasks`, `identity.users`, `qbank.question_versions`.
  - 1:N `submission_pages`.
  - Soft 1:1 `ai.marking_jobs` and 1:N `ai.gradings`.
- **Read/write:** pilot about 1,000 a day. Reads from the teacher queue and the student view.
- **Indexes:**
  - uq `(task_id, user_id, question_version_id)`.
  - `ix_sub_tenant_status (tenant_id, status, submitted_at)` serves the teacher queue, combined with the triage priority from AI.
  - `ix_sub_user (user_id, submitted_at DESC)`.
- **Risks:** cross-batch leakage. **Mitigation:** `tenant_id` filters, plus the B8 RLS and cross-tenant tests.
- **Build:** 6.

#### 4.C Accepted additions (exam, ai, institution)

All rows below are **accepted**.

| # | Addition | Table | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| PA-C1 | Add `district_id smallint`, `institution_id uuid`, `group_code text` and `version_lang text`, frozen at registration | `exam.registrations` | Merit scopes (district, institution, group) must not shift when a student edits their profile after the exam. EXAM may not read `identity.profiles` at ranking time, because that would be a cross-module query on 200k rows. |
| PA-C2 | Add `district_id`, `institution_id` and `group_code` | `exam.merit_entries` | Needed for the scoped rank indexes and for filtered merit-list reads without a join to another module. |
| PA-C3 | Add `tenant_id uuid` | `ai.gradings`, `ai.marking_jobs` | Defence-in-depth RLS in B8. Gradings currently inherit tenancy only through a polymorphic soft reference, which RLS cannot follow. |
| PA-C4 | Add `exam_id` to the PK prefix, or a local index `(exam_id, attempt_id, seq)` | `exam.exam_answer_batches` | The contiguity query groups by attempt within one exam; the index avoids a full scan of the month partition when several exams share it. Listed as `ix_eab_exam` above. |
| PA-C5 | Add `run_count smallint` | `exam.close_runs` | Reruns of a failed phase update the same row because of uq `(exam_id, phase)`; a counter makes reruns visible in the control room. |
| PA-C6 | Add `ends_at_effective` maintenance rule (no new column) | `exam.exams` | An extension must move `result_publish_at` forward to keep `ck_exams_publish_after_close` satisfied. This is a service rule, recorded here so the B5 build document includes it. |
