---

## 5. Cross-cutting database behaviour

### 5.1 Transaction boundaries

| Operation | Boundary | Isolation | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Save practice answer (B3) | One transaction: `attempt_answers` insert → `mistake_entries` recompute → `topic_progress` (node and ancestors) and `daily_study` upserts → **savepoint** → `daily_activity`, `reward_transactions`, `user_totals`, `streak_state` → release → `outbox` row → commit | READ COMMITTED with explicit row locks | If the GAM/ANL savepoint fails, roll back to the savepoint, enqueue `gam.repair_from_answer` (idempotent on the reward key), commit the answer and return `xp_awarded: 0, reward_pending: true`. Target: under 25 ms DB time p95 at Stage 1 **[Assumption]**. |
| Complete session (B3) | `practice_sessions` update + session-completed reward + outbox | READ COMMITTED | Reward key `xp:session_completed:{session_id}`. |
| Publish question version (B2) | `question_versions.status`, `questions.current_version_id`, `workflow_events`, `outbox(question.published)` | READ COMMITTED | Selectable only after the render and index jobs set `render_status` and `index_status` to `done`. |
| Key verification (B2) | Insert `key_verifications` + status transition if matched | SERIALIZABLE on the version row via `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` | Prevents two verifiers both "completing" verification. |
| Correction and rescoring batch (B3, B5) | The PRAC job `prac.rescore_correction`, triggered by `correction.created` from CMS. Each transaction covers 1,000 answers: `score_adjustments` + projections (ANL) + rewards (GAM) + the cursor in the job payload + outbox `rescoring.progress`. CMS consumes `rescoring.progress` and `rescoring.completed` to update `cms.corrections` in its own transaction (R-03: CMS never writes practice data or imports PRAC). | READ COMMITTED | Idempotent on `(user_id, answer_id, correction_id)`; resumable from the cursor. |
| Exam consumer batch (B5) | Up to 500 SQS messages: insert `exam_answer_batches` (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`) + upsert `exam_answers_latest` (`WHERE (excluded.seq, excluded.change_no) > (seq, change_no)`) + `exam_attempts.max_acked_seq = GREATEST(…, message.acked_through)` + outbox `exam.submitted` for submit markers | READ COMMITTED | SQS message deleted only after commit. |
| Exam final scoring (B5) | Per attempt chunk of 1,000: `exam_results` upsert + `attempt_answers` copies (for mastery) + outbox `exam.scored` | READ COMMITTED | Runs only after `close_runs(contiguity)` passed. |
| Merit snapshot (B5) | One transaction per snapshot: `merit_snapshots` + bulk `merit_entries` (`COPY`) | REPEATABLE READ snapshot of `exam_results` | Snapshot is immutable after commit. |
| Payment capture (B6) | `SELECT orders … FOR UPDATE` → verify amount → `payments` capture → `entitlements` + `entitlement_events` → `invoices` number → `outbox(payment.captured)` | READ COMMITTED | Unique `(provider, provider_txn_id)` blocks a double capture across processes. |
| Refund (B6) | Refund row → on provider confirm: entitlement shorten/revoke + events + credit note + outbox | READ COMMITTED | Partial refunds shorten only the refunded product's entitlement. |
| Tutor message (B4) | Tx 1: message row (`pending`) + idempotency key. Provider call outside any transaction. Tx 2: completion, citations, usage, quota charge, outbox. | READ COMMITTED | Never hold a transaction or a pooled connection across a provider call. |
| Social sign-in (B1) | Verify outside tx → tx: find or create `users` + `external_identities` + `device_sessions` + `refresh_tokens` + audit-security event | READ COMMITTED + unique `(provider, provider_subject)` | Concurrent first sign-ins race on the unique index; the loser re-reads and signs in to the winner's user. |
| Background job | Job's own writes + `processed_events` insert in one transaction | READ COMMITTED | Duplicate delivery hits the PK and the whole job transaction is a no-op. |
| Admin write (all) | Module change + `ops.audit_log` row in the same transaction via the `audited()` wrapper | READ COMMITTED | An admin write without an audit row is impossible by construction. |

### 5.2 Concurrency control and race conditions

| # | Race | Where | Control |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | Same answer synced twice concurrently from one device | `attempt_answers` | `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (user_id, id) DO NOTHING RETURNING`; on no row, read the stored row and return `duplicate: true`. |
| R2 | Two devices answer the same question at once | `mistake_entries` | `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` then `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on `(user_id, question_id)` before recomputing from attempts ordered by `effective_at`. |
| R3 | Daily XP cap crossed by parallel answers | `daily_activity` | Insert the row if missing, then `SELECT xp_from_answers … FOR UPDATE`; the service computes `awarded = max(0, min(x, cap − old))` and runs `UPDATE … SET xp_from_answers = xp_from_answers + awarded`. PostgreSQL `RETURNING` cannot return old values (R-19). The ledger row records the awarded amount. |
| R4 | Daily goal awarded twice | `reward_transactions` | Key `xp:daily_goal:{dhaka_date}` (built by `gam/contracts/reward_keys.py`), unique per user through `(user_id, idempotency_key)`. |
| R5 | Streak settlement races with a late answer at 06:00 | `streak_days`, `streak_state` | One `settle_through` function, run lazily in the answer savepoint and by the 06:00 job for inactive users, always under the per-user `streak_state` lock (`FOR UPDATE`). Days are settled in date order; PK on `streak_days`. Answers for a settled day do not alter it (C12 final settlement at P0; provisional days for offline-pack users from B7, S-9). |
| R6 | Freeze count above 2 | `streak_state` | `CHECK (freezes_held BETWEEN 0 AND 2)` plus `LEAST(2, freezes_held + 1)` on grant. |
| R7 | Two verifiers complete verification together | `key_verifications`, `question_versions` | Version row lock; uq `(question_version_id, verifier_id)`; `CHECK (verifier_id <> author_id)`. |
| R8 | Publish while a correction is in progress | `questions.current_version_id` | Version transitions are a state machine checked under the question row lock; `supersedes_id` must equal the current published version. |
| R9 | Double capture from webhook + callback + sweeper | `orders`, `payments` | Order row lock; uq `(provider, provider_txn_id)`; capture is a no-op if `orders.status = 'paid'`. |
| R10 | bKash execute called twice (one execute per payment id) | `payments` | Under the order lock, move `initiated → execute_requested` and commit **before** calling Execute; only that transition may lead to an Execute call. `execute_requested` or `execute_unknown` is resolved only by status query. A second successful payment on a paid order becomes `duplicate_captured` and is refunded automatically. |
| R11 | Coupon over-redeemed | `coupons`, `coupon_redemptions` | PK `(coupon_id, user_id)`; `UPDATE coupons SET redeemed_count = redeemed_count + 1 WHERE id = :id AND redeemed_count < max_redemptions RETURNING`. |
| R12 | Same exam batch delivered twice by SQS, or resent by the client | `exam_answer_batches`, ingest ack ledger | PK includes `(attempt_id, seq)` where `seq` is the batch number; `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`. Ingest re-acks `seq ≤ acked` without a new send and rejects `seq > acked + 1`. Latest table upsert guarded by `(excluded.seq, excluded.change_no) > (seq, change_no)`. |
| R13 | Device takeover during a live exam | `device_sessions.exam_epoch`, `exam_attempts.epoch` | Takeover increments epoch in one transaction; ingest rejects tokens with a stale epoch (epoch cached in the exam Redis on change); the new device refreshes its token via `…/attempts/current/token`. |
| R21 | Registrations racing at the cap edge | `exam.exams.registered_count` | Conditional `UPDATE … WHERE registered_count < registration_cap RETURNING`; release decrements in the same transaction as the status change (R-20). |
| R14 | Two job workers claim the same job | `platform.jobs` | `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` on `status = 'queued'`, set `status = 'running'` + `locked_until` lease; a crashed worker's lease expires and the job is re-claimed; handlers are idempotent via `processed_events` (per job or per relay batch). |
| R22 | A finished keyed job re-enqueued by an hourly scheduler (duplicate reminder or settlement) | `platform.jobs` | Keyed jobs are kept as `done` until `dedupe_until`, so the unique `dedupe_key` still blocks a re-enqueue within the window (R-18). |
| R15 | Refresh token reuse (stolen token) | `refresh_tokens` | Rotation in a transaction with `FOR UPDATE`; presenting a rotated token revokes the whole `family_id`. |
| R16 | Concurrent first social sign-in for the same identity | `external_identities` | Unique `(provider, provider_subject)`; loser catches the unique violation and re-reads. |
| R17 | Tutor quota charged twice on retry | Redis quota counter + `tutor_messages` | Quota charged once in Tx 2 only when status becomes `answered`, keyed by message id (`SETNX quota_charged:{message_id}`), and reconciled nightly against `ai_usage`. |
| R18 | Seat or sponsorship over-allocation | `seat_allocations`, `sponsorships` | Conditional `UPDATE … WHERE seats_used < seats_total RETURNING` plus the `CHECK`. |
| R19 | Invoice number gaps or duplicates | `invoice_sequences` | `UPDATE … SET last_no = last_no + 1 RETURNING` inside the capture transaction (row lock serialises; low volume). |
| R20 | Leaderboard drift after Redis loss, a retried job or a crash after commit | Redis boards | `ZINCRBY` is never gated by `processed_events` alone, because that is not atomic with Redis (R-16). Each board is applied by a Lua script holding a per-board marker `lb:marker:{board}` = the last applied ledger position `(recorded_at, id)`. The script skips entries at or below the marker and advances it in the same atomic step as `ZINCRBY`, so a retry double-counts nothing and a crash only delays. Fan-out is 9–12 boards per reward. A rebuild job recomputes any board from the ledger. |

### 5.3 Idempotency mechanisms

| Mechanism | Scope | Table |
|---|---|---|
| Device-generated `answer_id`, unique per user | Practice answers | `practice.attempt_answers` PK |
| `(attempt_id, seq)` | Exam batches | `exam.exam_answer_batches` PK |
| Reward idempotency key `kind:reason:source_id` | Every reward | `rewards.reward_transactions` uq |
| `Idempotency-Key` header with request hash | All other POSTs, including tutor messages, orders, refunds, admin actions | `platform.idempotency_keys` |
| `(consumer, event_id)` | Every background consumer | `platform.processed_events` |
| `(provider, provider_event_id)` and `(provider, provider_txn_id)` | Webhooks, captures | `commerce.provider_events`, `commerce.payments` |
| `(user_id, client_session_id)` | Session creation retries | `practice.practice_sessions` uq |
| `dedupe_key` + `dedupe_until` | Scheduled jobs (one settlement per user per day, one reminder per user per day). Keyed jobs stay as `done` until `dedupe_until`, so a finished job still blocks a re-enqueue within its window. | `platform.jobs` uq partial |
| `(consumer, relay_batch_id)` | Batched consumer jobs: the relay gives each consumer job a deterministic batch id `first_event_id:last_event_id`; per-event effects inside the batch stay idempotent through ledger keys and board markers | `platform.processed_events` |

Same key with a different request body returns `422 idempotency_key_reused`. Keys are scoped by user so one user's key can never return another user's response.

### 5.4 Consistency guarantees

| Data | Guarantee |
|---|---|
| Answer, notebook, progress, XP, streak | Atomic with the answer (C8), except when the GAM savepoint fails; then XP arrives within the repair job's retry window (seconds) and the response says so. |
| Leaderboards, notifications, event log, search index, item statistics | Eventually consistent from the outbox: **p95 ≤ 5 s** for boards and notifications **[Assumption, measured in the B3 benchmark]**; under 5 min for the S3 log. The relay polls every 250 ms while there is work and every 1 s when idle, over a direct (non-PgBouncer) connection. |
| Exam answers | Durable on SQS before acknowledgement; in Postgres within consumer lag; final results only after the contiguity check. |
| Entitlements | Strongly consistent in Postgres; cached per user in Redis (`pay:access:{user_id}`, 30 s TTL), deleted on `entitlement.changed` so every process sees the change. A refund or revocation takes effect within 30 s even if the invalidation is lost. |
| Cross-module references | Validated through interfaces at write time; nightly orphan checks per module report any reference to a missing row. |

### 5.5 Audit logging

- Admin writes: `ops.audit_log` through the `audited(actor, action, target, reason)` context manager (B1). Before and after images are the changed columns only, with PII fields redacted to their hashes.
- Security events (sign-in, token reuse, social link and unlink, OTP lockout, consent changes) are written to `ops.audit_log` with `module='auth'` and actor = the user.
- Financial history is self-auditing: `payments`, `refunds`, `entitlement_events`, `reward_transactions` and `referral_credits` are append-only or state-machine tables with no deletes.
- The application database role has `INSERT, SELECT` only on `ops.audit_log`; a separate migration role owns the table.

### 5.6 Data retention and deletion **[Assumption, pending D4 legal review]**

| Data | Retention | Mechanism |
|---|---|---|
| `outbox` | 7 days after relay | Drop daily partitions |
| `processed_events` | 14 days | Drop daily partitions |
| `idempotency_keys` | 7 days | Batched delete (10,000 rows per statement) |
| `otp_challenges` | 30 days | Batched delete |
| `jobs` (unkeyed completed jobs deleted on completion; keyed `done` jobs deleted after `dedupe_until`), `dead_jobs` | `dead_jobs` 90 days after resolution | Batched delete by the hourly retention job |
| Tutor threads and messages | 12 months, or until the student deletes (v2 §4.6) | Thread soft delete removes text within 24 h; monthly partitions dropped at 13 months |
| `ai_usage` | 24 months | Drop partitions |
| Photo-solve images | 30 days, then deleted; OCR text kept with the thread | Lifecycle rule on the bucket + `image_deleted_at` |
| Handwriting submission pages | Until the batch is archived + 12 months | Job; evaluation-set copies need separate consent (D4) |
| Notifications | 6 months | Drop partitions |
| SMS log | 24 months (cost audit) | Drop partitions |
| Exam answer batches and latest answers | 12 months after the merit list is final, then Parquet archive on S3 for 5 years | Archive job then drop monthly partition |
| `ops.audit_log` | 3 years | Drop partitions |
| Payments, refunds, invoices | 7 years (tax practice **[Needs evidence]**) | Never deleted by jobs |
| Attempts, rewards, results | Life of the account | Anonymised on deletion |
| Deleted accounts | Anonymise within 30 days: PII columns nulled, `phone_hash`/`email_hash` replaced, display name removed, external identities deleted, Apple token revoked (B8), files deleted; learning and financial records keep the pseudonymous `user_id` | `data_requests` workflow (support-driven at P0, automated in B9) |

### 5.7 Connection pooling

- **PgBouncer** in transaction mode in front of RDS. Application pools: `api` 10 connections per process × processes, `workers` 5, `ai-gateway` 5, `exam-consumers` 10. PgBouncer `default_pool_size` sized so the RDS `max_connections` (for example 1,000 on a large instance) is never exceeded at the Stage 4 process count.
- **asyncpg with PgBouncer:** `statement_cache_size=0` in the SQLAlchemy `connect_args`, or PgBouncer ≥ 1.21 with `max_prepared_statements=200` (preferred once verified in staging). Session-level features (`SET`, advisory session locks, `LISTEN`) are not used through PgBouncer; RLS tenant context uses `SET LOCAL` inside the transaction.
- **Separate pools:** migrations and partition maintenance connect directly to RDS with the owner role; the read-replica pool (Stage 3) is a second SQLAlchemy engine used only by declared read-only queries.
- **Timeouts:** `statement_timeout` 5 s for `api`, 30 s for workers, 2 s for the answer transaction's statements; `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout` 10 s; `lock_timeout` 2 s.

### 5.8 Backup and disaster recovery (NFR-10)

| Item | Target |
|---|---|
| Point-in-time recovery | RDS continuous archiving; RPO 5 min, RTO 1 h (v2 §8.5) |
| Snapshots | Daily, copied to a second region; 35-day retention |
| Object storage | S3 and R2 versioning; event log and exam archives with Object Lock (compliance mode, 1 year) |
| Exam answers in flight | SQS retention 4 days covers a full database restore during an exam window |
| Restore drill | Quarterly, timed, into an isolated account; verify row counts, a projection rebuild sample and `alembic current` |
| Redis | Not backed up for correctness; rebuild jobs (§3.17) rerun after loss |

### 5.9 Read replicas and partitioning strategy

- **Stage 1–2:** primary only, plus one replica reserved for search and reporting reads (v2 §7.1 search on a replica).
- **Stage 3 trigger:** primary CPU above 60% at peak for two weeks, or read latency p95 above budget. Then route declared read-only endpoints (dashboards, exam history, leaderboard rebuilds, bank search, analytics reads) to replicas with a staleness budget of 1 s; never the answer path, entitlements or anything read-your-writes.
- **Stage 4:** consider moving `practice` and `rewards` to their own RDS cluster (they share the answer transaction, so they move together); hash partitions already allow that move without rewriting keys. Exam answer tables move to their own instance only if exam-day write load measured in rehearsal exceeds the primary's budget.

### 5.10 Expected data growth **[Assumption]**

| Table | Rows per day, Year 1 (60k DAU) | Rows per day, Stage 4 (≈1M DAU) | Row size | Year-1 size |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `attempt_answers` | 1.8M (30 answers each) | 30M | ~350 B + indexes ~700 B | ~0.7 TB incl. indexes |
| `reward_transactions` | 3.6M | 60M | ~200 B | ~0.4 TB |
| `topic_progress` | updates only; ~2M rows total | ~40M rows | ~120 B | small |
| `tutor_messages` | 110k | 2M | ~1.5 KB | ~60 GB/year, dropped at 13 months |
| `notifications` | 120k | 2M | ~500 B | dropped at 6 months |
| `exam_answer_batches` | 20M per 200k exam | same per exam | ~600 B | ~12 GB per national exam, archived |
| `outbox` | ~6M | ~100M | ~600 B | daily partitions, 7-day window ~25 GB at Stage 1 |

### 5.11 High-volume read and write patterns

Rates are taken from the single derived table in [`17`](17_scalability_and_performance.md) §2.2. If the two ever differ, 17 wins.

| Path | Pattern | Stage 1 (1k conc.) | Stage 4 (100k conc.) | Design response |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Answer save | 6–9 statements, 1 transaction | ~17 tx/s | ~1,700 tx/s sustained, ~2,500 peak | 64 hash partitions; per-user rows only (no global hot row); no FKs on the two hot tables (§1.1); savepoint; PgBouncer |
| Session start | Read 6–10 published versions + content | ~1.3/s | ~130/s | Version payloads cached in Redis by `question_version_id` (immutable, so no invalidation) |
| Dashboard | Read `topic_progress` for one user | ~5/s | ~500/s | PK prefix scan; per-user cache 30 s |
| Leaderboard view | Redis only | ~5/s | ~500/s | No database query |
| Tutor message | 2 short transactions + provider call | ~0.8/s | ~83/s | Connection released during the provider call |
| Outbox relay | Batched read and mark | ~25 events/s | ~2,500 events/s | Partial index on unrelayed rows; batches of 1,000; polls every 250 ms while there is work; parallel relays by partition if needed |
| Exam ingest | Redis + SQS only | — | up to 30k req/s at 200k candidates | No Postgres on the ingest path |

### 5.12 Bottlenecks identified before implementation

| Bottleneck | Risk | Mitigation and owner build |
|---|---|---|
| Outbox relay throughput at Stage 4 | Lag in leaderboards and the event log; one job per event would be ~5,000 jobs/s at 100k concurrent, five times v2's Postgres-queue limit | **[Decision]** The relay creates **one consumer job per batch of up to 500 events per consumer** (about 15 job inserts/s at Stage 4), not one job per event; partition-aware parallel relay (B1 design, load-tested B9); split to SQS if job creation still exceeds ~1,000/s (v2 §0.2) |
| `jobs` table bloat | Autovacuum falls behind under high churn | Unkeyed completed jobs deleted on completion, keyed ones after `dedupe_until`; batched consumer jobs keep inserts near 15/s; aggressive per-table autovacuum settings; fill factor 70 |
| HNSW index build time and memory | Slow publishing at 150k questions × 2 languages | Build in a worker; `maintenance_work_mem` raised for the index job; embeddings computed in batch (B4) |
| Exam-day upsert rate (~19k rows/s at the 200k ceiling, architecture review §4.4) | Primary saturation during an exam | Consumers batch 500 messages per transaction; rehearsal measures it (B5, B9); exam tables can move to a dedicated instance |
| Rescoring a popular question (millions of answers) | Long-running load | Batches of 5,000 with cursor; throttled worker queue; off-peak scheduling (B3) |
| Leaderboard rebuild for all-time boards | Redis memory and rebuild time | All-time boards per track only; rebuild streams the ledger by partition (B3) |
